  The letter Z in this word is pronounced as the sound 'ts', which is not the typical English pronunciation of this letter or its common combinations.

  Hi Adam, how you doin' ?.
  From my point of view you build and gain vocabulary  by practicing a lot, well after studying Grammar and Phonetics you gotta speak the language constantly all the time, I speak from experience 'cause that's what I did when I started learning the language, besides it's the only efficient and effective way to learn a foreign language correctly and it's scientifically proven, it might take some time but that depends on how interested you are, how much effort you invest and how much time you put into it but I think it's worth it
  
  Well I think that what helped me the most is the fact that I had the chance to travel to the USA and lived over there for almost ten years and I had practicing, but that was a long time ago.

  A lot of problems for learners of English are caused by the peculiar rules of English punctuation: for instance, in Russian and in many other languages all the clauses within a complex sentence must be separated with commas, which makes it a lot easier to see where a clause starts and where it ends. The intonation, when you read such a sentence, is also based on the commas that you see between the clauses. The sentence given here as an example should have at least 7 (seven!) commas if written properly in Russian.

  What you called a "gerund phrase" is actually a non-finite (present participle) nominal clause which functions as the subject of the whole sentence.
    ↳ **@phumkiatwananuraksakul7815** (👍0 · 2025-08-12) — https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=JsEAcLPnWso&lc=UgyyMJhEb6GojHQYmml4AaABAg.A9hyloqTSpbAHrfAg7YC5L
      Gerund is called a verbal as a noun -like,but a non-finite verb is Ving that acts as an adjective called present participle,not a gerund.
      I think you mixed up between gerund and present participle that are different parts of speech.

    ↳ **@phumkiatwananuraksakul7815** (👍0 · 2025-08-12) — https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=JsEAcLPnWso&lc=Ugy4yX-hA_SH05s2tCZ4AaABAg.ADS3o5xa2EAAHrh5Gbojxl
      You don't understand what he explained because it is two kinds of particles,in fact 3 ones,but focus on this
      1.Having v.3 (also called perfect participle)...or reduced adverb clause of time or reason.,s+V2
      2.having (also called present participle)nouns as objects or complement .
